Take a look at this video to see what I mean Offline Chief Cook and Bottle Washer If you see a piece of truth glistening in the dirt, get down on your knees and gently dig it out, for it's the greatest treasure you will ever find Posts: 3576 Thank you received: 14517 Homework: This will be alot like the Read a Session Homework, except that I want you to do learn from it how to pinpoint in advance, turns in the market which will pay you very large rewards for minimal risk. HOME So EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Open a D1 chart on any pair Download as much LTF history as you can for it Start at the second price turn on the left Look to the left of it, in the lowest TF you can Work out why price reacted to that level Mark the reason on the LTF chart Take a little screenshot of that area - name it "name of pair"1 Mark the turning point on D1 with a 1 Move on to the next turning point, and repeat, and then the next. Do this for every turning point. When you're finished, you'll have a D1 chart with all the turning points numbered, and a gallery of thumbnails of the LTFs, each explaining the turns. Do this for as many pairs as you possibly can Believe me, it'll be well worth it! Trader Posts: 133 Harry's comment that I posted ended up in my journal because I was Thank you received: confused, so take heart because you are not alone. My (less than) 2 cents worth and suggestions for you: 231 1. Like Lupy said, treat this like an interest you're investigating right now, as if it were mountain biking, knitting, or photography, but DO NOT worry about what you do or do not understand right now or who is or is not making money - you wouldn't be worried about every detail, method, or maneuver if you were thinking about taking up biking, knitting, or photos, so the same applies here. 2. Forget terminology for now and just think of it this way: price can only go up or down, right? (It never truly goes sideways, so forget that for now.) Yes, price goes up or down. Before it goes up or down, the market makes a decision and that decision is what sends it up or down. Do not allow yourself to complicate it with terms for now! If price went up, the market made a decision. And if price went down, the market made the opposite decision. Nothing more, nothing less. 3. Perhaps you think I'm oversimplifying. OK, then disregard everything that I've said and watch this video by If and read and reread the following words as many times as necessary until you believe it: "... decisions, that's all HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT supply and demand is and that's all that support and resistance are. It's just decisions on the value of an asset." ~ Ifmyante in the video linked above 4. Once you've heard, read, and watched the video in #3 enough times to believe it like you believe the sun is hot, look at the charts below and keep everything simple and see if you follow as I try to "Find the Decisions" as this thread's title says. I'm trying to A. find an engulf; then B. mark where I think the decision was made to create that engulf; then C. watch what happens the first time back to the price where the decision was made. ABC - we've got this! Post your questions, find the ABC's on your charts and know that the RTM community stands ready to help you learn but please: don't give up! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Nasir, bulbuli, pauga, Ahmadhassan745, Kadox, salivan, alirezat, Nbr42, cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Find the Decisions 13 Jul 2014 05:57 #227 fxdaytrader There are decisions all over the chart - it does not make them tradeable or qualifies as a system. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Offline Flips, fresh S/D. FL's, QM's, FTRs. tips of CP etc etc - yes, you will always find a reason why price turns (just keep looking left - a bit further, further...). Trader Again, it still does not mean you will be consistently profitable identifying them. Posts: 51 Sometimes the decision will appear on a 1M and sometimes it will be Thank you received: 72 "obvious" on a daily. Sometimes the "decision" will be 50 pips apart dependent upon the timeframe. Again - good luck making money with it. But after the event - you will look like a genius FTB to a DP with CP on approach - yes - sounds good, but watch how many fail. Suddenly, you realise this is a game of probabilities as much as this site and others seem to promote something far more certain. Suddenly you realise RR, trade management, HTF context, time of day, volatility, account size and other variables are much more important than this site and most give credit to. Everyone can keep getting excited when price turns to the pip, because on a 1M there was a flip zone, but I guarantee you - it will just continue to perpetuate the retailer dream. Just because anyone keeps posting pip precise trades (after the event of course) that just never lose, please accept that they are not showing their losers. Of course there are many that do not have the experience to appreciate this reality and will dismiss this post, thinking I am just another frustrated losing trader. Maybe I am, maybe I am not. But for all the losing traders here, I dare you to come back in 2 years and read this post again and tell me I am wrong. This post is in no way directed to polymorph, and I am not suggesting that finding decisions is not part of the process, but the fascination with pip perfect entries will ultimately distract you from the real objective which is to consistently make $. I love trading and really respect both new and experienced traders for having a go in the market, but eventually the insanity has to stop. GL all. Do you have a better path to understanding or are Thank you received: you just saying it can't be done and we should just give up as fxyogi suggested he'd like to? Or are you saying something else entirely? 231 Maybe someday "to the pip" entries will be possible for me, but I'm definitely not there yet and hope that I didn't give the impression that I am. Also, people often refer to "the Holy Grail" as if it means never being wrong again. The Holy Grail of trading is profitability - it's what eludes most traders and is the measure of success, not being right 100% of the time because that's impossible. I've seen some losers posted (not very many of them for sure) and If posted a summary of his trading and success rates recently in RTMA thread and in RTM on FF. He didn't have 100% winners but he was quite profitable. Again, I can't outright disagree with most of what you said, just not sure how an aspiring trader is supposed to apply your comments regarding the unreliability of "Flips, fresh S/D. FL's, QM's, FTRs. tips of CP etc... just keep looking left" on a site that says those things are reliable and in a thread that's devoted to finding them on a chart. It didn't take me long to realize the facts this post points out. After reading hundreds of post on this site, I've been wondering why nobody had dared to expose to cold truth. While I find fascinating how price turns on certain points, I also came to realize that it's just matter of probability. Anyway, I don't want to give a lecture here, just wanted to highlight this post as the most important post on this whole site which should be included at the top of the Marketpedia. Maybe anyone with just a bit of brains and some time to test live, will come to this same conclusion. Risk free account application: HOME www.the5ers.com FORUM MORE LOGOUT EDUCATION QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Nosbigs Find the Decisions homework Due 2/28/2013 16 Sep 2012 12:06 #62 Nosbigs This will be alot like the Read a Session Homework, except that I want you to do learn from it how to pinpoint in advance, turns in the market which will pay you very large rewards for minimal risk. Visitor So Open a D1 chart on any pair Download as much LTF history as you can for it Start at the second price turn on the left Look to the left of it, in the lowest TF you can Work out why price reacted to that level Mark the reason on the LTF chart Take a little screenshot of that area - name it "name of pair"1 Mark the turning point on D1 with a 1 Move on to the next turning point, and repeat, and then the next. Do this for every turning point. When you're finished, you'll have a D1 chart with all the turning points numbered, and a gallery of thumbnails of the LTFs, each explaining the turns. Do this for as many pairs as you possibly can Believe me, it'll be well worth it! Posts: 487 I am trying to find the right words to explain what a decision point is to Thank you received: make it easy for you to understand... 1115 So maybe this might help you a little bit: What is a decision point: Well it's a price point on a chart where a decision has been made to take price either higher or lower Where can I spot a decision point ? You will see that new decision points take place at Unfresh Supply or Demand areas. Let's take a Supply area for example. New supply is created because of a new decision point. Now to take this supply away and break it , the big boys will need to make a decision and put lots of money in the market to take the supply out. So you will see that unfresh demand , eventually becomes Fresh Supply once it gets broken. This happens because all the demand was consumed (multiple retest of the area) and that there was finally a decision made to take price lower. Hope this makes sence, if not let me know and I'll try to explain it beter. Best, Mel HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Oedipus99, mehrdad, wongpasar, Paichayon, ruedevalence, Ahmadhassan745, salivan, staurum, alirezat, cuimingzhao, traderscape, MDY A positive mind finds a way it can be done. Posts: 941 Thank you received: 2920 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT now for your question, my view but better have a look on IF's chart, open a blank one of yours and start marking them. when i first saw it, i was looking at it like stupid for a while. opened and closed it many times because i couldnt mark anything on mine. then after a while it was easier to see. start from something that breaks maybe as its easier to spot, and take it from there. do decision point hold forever? Attachments: v=ov9kH54Z- dM&desktop_uri=%2Fwatch%3Fv%3Dov9kH54Z- dM Posts: 327 Thank you received: 390 The following user(s) said Thank You: strading, HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Find the Decisions 08 Jun 2013 22:28 #144 mettafort OK here are some more breakdowns of the decisions. The realization already is that price is only ever moving from one decision point to another, and the invariant at any decision point (DP) is value. If price falls away from an area, its decided price is too expensive - this is the invariant at this level at this moment. The basis to Offline that invariant is the amount of supply that such a DP attracts. After the decision is made, different Trader market participants may then join in, not until, adding more supply and helping the invariant hold If I were a song...http://m.youtube.com/watch? when price returns - RTM style trading does v=ov9kH54Z- exactly that. dM&desktop_uri=%2Fwatch%3Fv%3Dov9kH54Z- dM The core move that we see is a move up into a Posts: 327 space we can reason about, which is, if upwards, Thank you received: 390 an DP area where it was previously decided price was too expensive. There will probably be resting limit orders, as well as active traders watching as price returns to the area. Everyone is waiting for some confluence depending on their trading modality, but at some point the supply/demand invariant holds or it breaks. So the move up happens, and the sign that price is too expensive is the engulf down. Some engulfs matter more than others it seems, and if a key DP is engulfed, price ascends in the winds of demand that it consumed, until those winds fade out and price returns to that last place where supply was overwhelming. This is the FTB trade down. And of course, the key to the DP is how the decision was made - was it timid, or was there conviction. But the primary takeaway is that price is bouncing between decisions on value. One cannot say that its just some invariant that holds until price at a certain level in the book hits zero, because it is dropping as well as rising as the dynamics of the decisions about value play out. Posts: 72 Dear FXyogi....i have had more than a few failed trades lately ...and started Thank you received: to question myself ...then i remembered o quote i have read somewhere that said something like "When you experience failure, it is really 233 success letting you know that you have identified a flaw that needs to be corrected or removed" so i started to analyze every failed trade and drawn the conclusion that i'm not one with the market yet but most important that my targets were too optimistic.Every failed trade i had was in profit until the first trouble area...where of course it didn't engulf and continued in the original direction....I suggest you to do the same with your trades. First of all it is my thinking that RTM doesn't teach us a method but gives us the decryption keys of what once seemed an encrypted chaos.It is up to us to develop a method based on what we learn....and of course develop the all needed discipline .Think of this if you only searched for your very very best 1:3 setups and took them ...with only 200 trades with 2% risk you add 5 zeros to your account.(thats 100.000.000 from 1000).I'm not saying you will not have losses but what's the point of taking lame and shady trades if you can wait for the very best setups (like Harry said"i don't go on blind dates in dark alleys " )..I'm writing this to encourage you to proceed on your journey . I know that watching IF's charts and trades and other members it may give you the confidence that RTM setups are fail safe ...BUT they are safe untill the next decision point ...further it's just a gamble if we don't get PA(engulf).And they have spent maybe 10000 s of hours before they trade like that. You need to lose the mentality of trading systems preached everywhere on the net ...you can't have rules...more like guidelines .....price is a story with a plot that builds and changes constantly upon earlier events.....all we can do is use what we learned here (FL,FTR,Engulf ,Fo) and if all the holes line up in the swiss cheese then we take the trade...but using the appropriate risk apetite.And if the story is unclear stay out....a good trader isn't the sum of all the trades he takes....but how he adapts to the environment he's in without "bleeding" too much . Maybe you should treat this as a hobby at first so that you don't feel the pressure to succeed.Allocate a few hours daily or weekly to learn .Take every master elite and associate trader posts from the beginning on this forum and read them...save the charts and save their words of wisdom .I write them with pen and paper...i think they sink in better this way.And when you have finished take all your quotes and organize them into the categories found in Marketpedia .And then start in the homework ....Now this is one of the highly profitable business on earth...do you think it can be done without some sort of leap of faith?or sweat?... But you need to set your priorities straight. Take a look at this video to see what I mean Offline Chief Cook and Bottle Washer If you see a piece of truth glistening in the dirt, get down on your knees and gently dig it out, for it's the greatest treasure you will ever find Posts: 3576 Thank you received: 14517 Homework: This will be alot like the Read a Session Homework, except that I want you to do learn from it how to pinpoint in advance, turns in the market which will pay you very large rewards for minimal risk. HOME So EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Open a D1 chart on any pair Download as much LTF history as you can for it Start at the second price turn on the left Look to the left of it, in the lowest TF you can Work out why price reacted to that level Mark the reason on the LTF chart Take a little screenshot of that area - name it "name of pair"1 Mark the turning point on D1 with a 1 Move on to the next turning point, and repeat, and then the next. Do this for every turning point. When you're finished, you'll have a D1 chart with all the turning points numbered, and a gallery of thumbnails of the LTFs, each explaining the turns. Do this for as many pairs as you possibly can Believe me, it'll be well worth it! Trader Posts: 133 Harry's comment that I posted ended up in my journal because I was Thank you received: confused, so take heart because you are not alone. My (less than) 2 cents worth and suggestions for you: 231 1. Like Lupy said, treat this like an interest you're investigating right now, as if it were mountain biking, knitting, or photography, but DO NOT worry about what you do or do not understand right now or who is or is not making money - you wouldn't be worried about every detail, method, or maneuver if you were thinking about taking up biking, knitting, or photos, so the same applies here. 2. Forget terminology for now and just think of it this way: price can only go up or down, right? (It never truly goes sideways, so forget that for now.) Yes, price goes up or down. Before it goes up or down, the market makes a decision and that decision is what sends it up or down. Do not allow yourself to complicate it with terms for now! If price went up, the market made a decision. And if price went down, the market made the opposite decision. Nothing more, nothing less. 3. Perhaps you think I'm oversimplifying. OK, then disregard everything that I've said and watch this video by If and read and reread the following words as many times as necessary until you believe it: "... decisions, that's all HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT supply and demand is and that's all that support and resistance are. It's just decisions on the value of an asset." ~ Ifmyante in the video linked above 4. Once you've heard, read, and watched the video in #3 enough times to believe it like you believe the sun is hot, look at the charts below and keep everything simple and see if you follow as I try to "Find the Decisions" as this thread's title says. I'm trying to A. find an engulf; then B. mark where I think the decision was made to create that engulf; then C. watch what happens the first time back to the price where the decision was made. ABC - we've got this! Post your questions, find the ABC's on your charts and know that the RTM community stands ready to help you learn but please: don't give up! HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Offline Flips, fresh S/D. FL's, QM's, FTRs. tips of CP etc etc - yes, you will always find a reason why price turns (just keep looking left - a bit further, further...). Trader Again, it still does not mean you will be consistently profitable identifying them. Posts: 51 Sometimes the decision will appear on a 1M and sometimes it will be Thank you received: 72 "obvious" on a daily. Sometimes the "decision" will be 50 pips apart dependent upon the timeframe. Again - good luck making money with it. But after the event - you will look like a genius FTB to a DP with CP on approach - yes - sounds good, but watch how many fail. Suddenly, you realise this is a game of probabilities as much as this site and others seem to promote something far more certain. Suddenly you realise RR, trade management, HTF context, time of day, volatility, account size and other variables are much more important than this site and most give credit to. Everyone can keep getting excited when price turns to the pip, because on a 1M there was a flip zone, but I guarantee you - it will just continue to perpetuate the retailer dream. Just because anyone keeps posting pip precise trades (after the event of course) that just never lose, please accept that they are not showing their losers. Of course there are many that do not have the experience to appreciate this reality and will dismiss this post, thinking I am just another frustrated losing trader. Maybe I am, maybe I am not. But for all the losing traders here, I dare you to come back in 2 years and read this post again and tell me I am wrong. This post is in no way directed to polymorph, and I am not suggesting that finding decisions is not part of the process, but the fascination with pip perfect entries will ultimately distract you from the real objective which is to consistently make $. I love trading and really respect both new and experienced traders for having a go in the market, but eventually the insanity has to stop. GL all. This is a hard business and though I don't take any offense at your comments or take them Offline personal, I've got to wonder what the takeaway is from your post. This thread is filled with posts about finding decisions but your 2nd paragraph Trader seems to indicate that no amount of finding decisions and applying that knowledge in the future will help you understand the markets and profit Posts: 133 from that understanding. Do you have a better path to understanding or are Thank you received: you just saying it can't be done and we should just give up as fxyogi suggested he'd like to? Or are you saying something else entirely? 231 Maybe someday "to the pip" entries will be possible for me, but I'm definitely not there yet and hope that I didn't give the impression that I am. Also, people often refer to "the Holy Grail" as if it means never being wrong again. The Holy Grail of trading is profitability - it's what eludes most traders and is the measure of success, not being right 100% of the time because that's impossible. I've seen some losers posted (not very many of them for sure) and If posted a summary of his trading and success rates recently in RTMA thread and in RTM on FF. He didn't have 100% winners but he was quite profitable. Again, I can't outright disagree with most of what you said, just not sure how an aspiring trader is supposed to apply your comments regarding the unreliability of "Flips, fresh S/D. FL's, QM's, FTRs. tips of CP etc... just keep looking left" on a site that says those things are reliable and in a thread that's devoted to finding them on a chart. Risk free account application: HOME www.the5ers.com FORUM MORE LOGOUT EDUCATION QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Nosbigs Find the Decisions homework Due 2/28/2013 16 Sep 2012 12:06 #62 Nosbigs This will be alot like the Read a Session Homework, except that I want you to do learn from it how to pinpoint in advance, turns in the market which will pay you very large rewards for minimal risk. Visitor So Open a D1 chart on any pair Download as much LTF history as you can for it Start at the second price turn on the left Look to the left of it, in the lowest TF you can Work out why price reacted to that level Mark the reason on the LTF chart Take a little screenshot of that area - name it "name of pair"1 Mark the turning point on D1 with a 1 Move on to the next turning point, and repeat, and then the next. Do this for every turning point. When you're finished, you'll have a D1 chart with all the turning points numbered, and a gallery of thumbnails of the LTFs, each explaining the turns. Do this for as many pairs as you possibly can Believe me, it'll be well worth it! Posts: 487 I am trying to find the right words to explain what a decision point is to Thank you received: make it easy for you to understand... 1115 So maybe this might help you a little bit: What is a decision point: Well it's a price point on a chart where a decision has been made to take price either higher or lower Where can I spot a decision point ? You will see that new decision points take place at Unfresh Supply or Demand areas. Let's take a Supply area for example. New supply is created because of a new decision point. Now to take this supply away and break it , the big boys will need to make a decision and put lots of money in the market to take the supply out. So you will see that unfresh demand , eventually becomes Fresh Supply once it gets broken. This happens because all the demand was consumed (multiple retest of the area) and that there was finally a decision made to take price lower. Hope this makes sence, if not let me know and I'll try to explain it beter. Best, Mel HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: Oedipus99, mehrdad, wongpasar, Paichayon, ruedevalence, Ahmadhassan745, salivan, staurum, alirezat, cuimingzhao, traderscape, MDY A positive mind finds a way it can be done. Posts: 941 Thank you received: 2920 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT now for your question, my view but better have a look on IF's chart, open a blank one of yours and start marking them. when i first saw it, i was looking at it like stupid for a while. opened and closed it many times because i couldnt mark anything on mine. then after a while it was easier to see. start from something that breaks maybe as its easier to spot, and take it from there. do decision point hold forever? Attachments: v=ov9kH54Z- dM&desktop_uri=%2Fwatch%3Fv%3Dov9kH54Z- dM Posts: 327 Thank you received: 390 The following user(s) said Thank You: strading, HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT cuimingzhao THANK YOU QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE Find the Decisions 08 Jun 2013 22:28 #144 mettafort OK here are some more breakdowns of the decisions. The realization already is that price is only ever moving from one decision point to another, and the invariant at any decision point (DP) is value. If price falls away from an area, its decided price is too expensive - this is the invariant at this level at this moment. The basis to Offline that invariant is the amount of supply that such a DP attracts. After the decision is made, different Trader market participants may then join in, not until, adding more supply and helping the invariant hold If I were a song...http://m.youtube.com/watch? when price returns - RTM style trading does v=ov9kH54Z- exactly that. dM&desktop_uri=%2Fwatch%3Fv%3Dov9kH54Z- dM The core move that we see is a move up into a Posts: 327 space we can reason about, which is, if upwards, Thank you received: 390 an DP area where it was previously decided price was too expensive. There will probably be resting limit orders, as well as active traders watching as price returns to the area. Everyone is waiting for some confluence depending on their trading modality, but at some point the supply/demand invariant holds or it breaks. So the move up happens, and the sign that price is too expensive is the engulf down. Some engulfs matter more than others it seems, and if a key DP is engulfed, price ascends in the winds of demand that it consumed, until those winds fade out and price returns to that last place where supply was overwhelming. This is the FTB trade down. And of course, the key to the DP is how the decision was made - was it timid, or was there conviction. But the primary takeaway is that price is bouncing between decisions on value. One cannot say that its just some invariant that holds until price at a certain level in the book hits zero, because it is dropping as well as rising as the dynamics of the decisions about value play out. Posts: 72 Dear FXyogi....i have had more than a few failed trades lately ...and started Thank you received: to question myself ...then i remembered o quote i have read somewhere that said something like "When you experience failure, it is really 233 success letting you know that you have identified a flaw that needs to be corrected or removed" so i started to analyze every failed trade and drawn the conclusion that i'm not one with the market yet but most important that my targets were too optimistic.Every failed trade i had was in profit until the first trouble area...where of course it didn't engulf and continued in the original direction....I suggest you to do the same with your trades. First of all it is my thinking that RTM doesn't teach us a method but gives us the decryption keys of what once seemed an encrypted chaos.It is up to us to develop a method based on what we learn....and of course develop the all needed discipline .Think of this if you only searched for your very very best 1:3 setups and took them ...with only 200 trades with 2% risk you add 5 zeros to your account.(thats 100.000.000 from 1000).I'm not saying you will not have losses but what's the point of taking lame and shady trades if you can wait for the very best setups (like Harry said"i don't go on blind dates in dark alleys " )..I'm writing this to encourage you to proceed on your journey . I know that watching IF's charts and trades and other members it may give you the confidence that RTM setups are fail safe ...BUT they are safe untill the next decision point ...further it's just a gamble if we don't get PA(engulf).And they have spent maybe 10000 s of hours before they trade like that. You need to lose the mentality of trading systems preached everywhere on the net ...you can't have rules...more like guidelines .....price is a story with a plot that builds and changes constantly upon earlier events.....all we can do is use what we learned here (FL,FTR,Engulf ,Fo) and if all the holes line up in the swiss cheese then we take the trade...but using the appropriate risk apetite.And if the story is unclear stay out....a good trader isn't the sum of all the trades he takes....but how he adapts to the environment he's in without "bleeding" too much . Maybe you should treat this as a hobby at first so that you don't feel the pressure to succeed.Allocate a few hours daily or weekly to learn .Take every master elite and associate trader posts from the beginning on this forum and read them...save the charts and save their words of wisdom .I write them with pen and paper...i think they sink in better this way.And when you have finished take all your quotes and organize them into the categories found in Marketpedia .And then start in the homework ....Now this is one of the highly profitable business on earth...do you think it can be done without some sort of leap of faith?or sweat?... But you need to set your priorities straight.
